"{\"title\":\"Releases · openai/codex\",\"description\":\"Lightweight coding agent that runs in your terminal - Releases · openai/codex\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/openai/codex/releases\",\"content\":\"## rusty-v8-v147.4.0\\n\\n```\\nFix Darwin rusty-v8 runtime staging\\n```\\n\\n## 0.130.0-alpha.4\\n\\n## 0.130.0-alpha.3\\n\\n## 0.129.0\\n\\n## New Features\\n\\n- The TUI now supports modal Vim editing in the composer, including `/vim`, default-mode config, and Vim-specific keymap contexts. (#18595)\\n- TUI workflows are easier to resume and copy from with a redesigned resume/fork picker, raw scrollback mode, `/ide` context injection, and workspace-aware `/diff`. (#20065, #20819, #20294, #21001)\\n- The status line can show theme-aware colors plus optional PR and branch-change summaries, and `/keymap debug` helps inspect terminal key events. (#19631, #20892, #20794)\\n- Plugin management now supports workspace sharing, share access controls, source filtering, local share path tracking, marketplace removal/upgrades, remote bundle sync, and admin-disabled status handling. (#20278, #21124, #21419, #20560, #19843, #20478, #20268, #20298)\\n- Hooks can be browsed and toggled from `/hooks`, can run before/after compaction, and can add `PreToolUse` context; Codex Apps auth and eligible MCP elicitations now surface through TUI/Guardian flows. (#19882, #19905, #20692, #19193, #19431)\\n- Experimental goals are now discoverable, stay paused across resume unless the user opts back in, and show clearer validation and multi-day duration output. (#20083, #20790, #20746, #20558)\\n\\n## Bug Fixes\\n\\n- `/copy` works better in tmux, Alt+Enter and modified Delete/Backspace keys behave correctly, and Windows typing/paste latency was reduced. (#20207, #20535, #21058, #18914)\\n- Large paste placeholders and Ctrl+C-stashed drafts now survive clear/editor workflows without corrupting draft history. (#21091, #21190, #21351, #21397)\\n- TUI startup and accessibility were tightened by bounding terminal probes, clearing the first inline viewport render, and honoring `animations = false` for live rows. (#20654, #21450, #20564)\\n- Linux sandbox startup is more reliable across older `bwrap`, slow mount probes, symlink-protected paths, and shared `/tmp` setups. (#20628, #20111, #21127, #21234)\\n- Windows sandbox and exec policy now handle named pipes, ConPTY teardown, PowerShell-wrapped allow rules, worktree `safe.directory`, and unsafe Git options more reliably. (#20270, #20685, #20336, #21409, #21275)\\n- Fixed custom CA login behind TLS-inspecting proxies, Bedrock runtime endpoint reporting, dangerous project config keys, heredoc redirect approval matching, and unbounded MCP/hook output growth. (#20676, #20275, #20098, #20113, #20260, #21069)\\n\\n## Documentation\\n\\n- Updated the embedded OpenAI Docs sample skill so API-key setup guidance stays aligned with other docs variants. (#21263)\\n- Documented how generated git commit attribution is gated by `codex_git_commit` and configured in `config.toml`. (#21379)\\n- Removed local-only planning/spec docs and redirected config docs toward the maintained external documentation surface. (#20896)\\n\\n## Chores\\n\\n- Linux releases now build, publish, bundle, and verify a standalone `bwrap` fallback for npm and DotSlash installs. (#21255, #21256, #21257, #21312, #21285)\\n- Vendored Bubblewrap was updated to 0.11.2, including upstream security changes around setuid support. (#21389)\\n- Windows Bazel CI now uses faster cross-compilation for tests, clippy, and release-build checks, and Bazel now runs sharded Rust integration tests. (#20585, #20701, #21057)\\n- App-server and protocol internals were split and slimmed down, including transport extraction, protocol module decomposition, thread/message history moves, and tool-handler cleanup. (#20324, #20325, #20348, #20545, #21251, #21278, #21395)\\n- Analytics and diagnostics coverage expanded for tool lifecycles, goals, plugin skills, thread sources, service tiers, and PR issue labeling. ### Q6

"{\"title\":\"Windows – Codex app | OpenAI Developers\",\"description\":\"Use the Codex app on Windows with native sandbox and PowerShell support\",\"url\":\"https://developers.openai.com/codex/app/windows\",\"content\":\"The Codex app for Windows gives you one interface for\\nworking across projects, running parallel agent threads, and reviewing results.\\nThe Windows app supports core workflows such as worktrees, automations, Git\\nfunctionality, the in-app browser, artifact previews, plugins, and skills.\\nIt runs natively on Windows using PowerShell and the\\nWindows sandbox, or you can configure it to\\nrun in Windows Subsystem for Linux 2 (WSL2).\\n\\nDownload the Codex app from the\\nMicrosoft Store.\\n\\nThen follow the quickstart to get started.\\n\\nTo update the app, open the Microsoft Store, go to __Downloads__, and click\\n__Check for updates__. The Store installs the latest version afterward.\\n\\nFor enterprises, administrators can deploy the app with Microsoft Store app\\ndistribution through enterprise management tools.\\n\\nIf you prefer a command-line install path, or need an alternative to opening\\nthe Microsoft Store UI, run:\\n\\n```\\nwinget install Codex -s msstore\\n```\\n\\nThe Codex app on Windows supports a native Windows sandbox when the agent runs in PowerShell, and uses Linux sandboxing when you run the agent in Windows Subsystem for Linux 2 (WSL2). To apply sandbox protections in either mode, set sandbox permissions to __Default permissions__ in the Composer before sending messages to Codex.\\n\\nRunning Codex in full access mode means Codex is not limited to your project\\ndirectory and might perform unintentional destructive actions that can lead to\\ndata loss. Keep sandbox boundaries in place and use rules for\\ntargeted exceptions, or set your approval policy to\\nnever to have\\nCodex attempt to solve problems without asking for escalated permissions,\\nbased on your approval and security setup.\\n\\n### Preferred editor\\n\\nChoose a default app for __Open__, such as Visual Studio, VS Code, or another\\neditor. You can override that choice per project. If you already picked a\\ndifferent app from the __Open__ menu for a project, that project-specific\\nchoice takes precedence.\\n\\n### Integrated terminal\\n\\nYou can also choose the default integrated terminal. Depending on what you have\\ninstalled, options include:\\n\\n- PowerShell\\n- Command Prompt\\n- Git Bash\\n- WSL\\n\\nThis change applies only to new terminal sessions. If you already have an\\nintegrated terminal open, restart the app or start a new thread before\\nexpecting the new default terminal to appear.\\n\\nBy default, the Codex app uses the Windows-native agent. That means the agent\\nruns commands in PowerShell. The app can still work with projects that live in\\nWindows Subsystem for Linux 2 (WSL2) by using the `wsl` CLI when needed.\\n\\nIf you want to add a project from the WSL filesystem, click __Add new project__\\nor press `Ctrl`+`O`, then type `\\\\\\\\wsl$\\\\` into the File\\nExplorer window. From there, choose your Linux distribution and the folder you\\nwant to open.\\n\\nIf you plan to keep using the Windows-native agent, prefer storing projects on\\nyour Windows filesystem and accessing them from WSL through\\n`/mnt/<drive>/...`. This setup is more reliable than opening projects\\ndirectly from the WSL filesystem.\\n\\nIf you want the agent itself to run in WSL2, open __Settings__,\\nswitch the agent from Windows native to WSL, and __restart the app__. The\\nchange doesn’t take effect until you restart. Your projects should remain in\\nplace after restart.\\n\\nWSL1 was supported through Codex `0.114`. Starting in Codex `0.115`, the Linux\\nsandbox moved to `bubblewrap`, so WSL1 is no longer supported.\\n\\nYou configure the integrated terminal independently from the agent. See\\nCustomize for your dev setup for the\\nterminal options. You can keep the agent in WSL and still use PowerShell in the\\nterminal, or use WSL for both, depending on your workflow.\\n\\nCodex works best when a few common developer tools are already installed:\\n\\n- __Git__: Powers the review panel in the Codex app and lets you inspect or\\n  revert changes.\\n- __Node.js__: A common tool that the agent uses to perform tasks more\\n  efficiently.\\n- __Python__: A common tool that the agent uses to perform tasks more\\n  efficiently.\\n- __.NET SDK__: Useful when you want to build native Windows apps.\\n- __GitHub CLI__: Powers GitHub-specific functionality in the Codex app.\\n\\nInstall them with the default Windows package manager `winget` by pasting this\\ninto the integrated terminal or\\nasking Codex to install them:\\n\\n```\\nwinget install --id Git.Git\\nwinget install --id OpenJS.NodeJS.LTS\\nwinget install --id Python.Python.3.14\\nwinget install --id Microsoft.DotNet.SDK.10\\nwinget install --id GitHub.cli\\n```\\n\\nAfter installing GitHub CLI, run `gh auth login` to enable GitHub features in\\nthe app.\\n\\nIf you need a different Python or .NET version, change the package IDs to the\\nversion you want.\\n\\n### Run commands with elevated permissions\\n\\nIf you need Codex to run commands with elevated permissions, start the Codex app\\nitself as an administrator. After installation, open the Start menu, find\\nCodex, and choose Run as administrator. The Codex agent inherits that\\npermission level.\\n\\n### PowerShell execution policy blocks commands\\n\\nIf you have never used tools such as Node.js or `npm` in PowerShell before, the\\nCodex agent or integrated terminal may hit execution policy errors.\\n\\nThis can also happen if Codex creates PowerShell scripts for you. In that case,\\nyou may need a less restrictive execution policy before PowerShell will run\\nthem.\\n\\nAn error may look something like this:\\n\\n```\\nnpm.ps1 cannot be loaded because running scripts is disabled on this system.\\n```\\n\\nA common fix is to set the execution policy to `RemoteSigned`:\\n\\n```\\nSet-ExecutionPolicy -ExecutionPolicy RemoteSigned\\n```\\n\\nFor details and other options, check Microsoft’s\\nexecution policy guide\\nbefore changing the policy.\\n\\n### Local environment scripts on Windows\\n\\nIf your local environment uses cross-platform\\ncommands such as `npm` scripts, you can keep one shared setup script or\\nset of actions for every platform.\\n\\nIf you need Windows-specific behavior, create Windows-specific setup scripts or\\nWindows-specific actions.\\n\\nActions run in the environment used by your integrated terminal. See\\nCustomize for your dev setup.\\n\\nLocal setup scripts run in the agent environment: WSL if the agent uses WSL,\\nand PowerShell otherwise.\\n\\nThe Windows app uses the same Codex home directory as native Codex on Windows:\\n`%USERPROFILE%\\\\.codex`.\\n\\nIf you also run the Codex CLI inside WSL, the CLI uses the Linux home\\ndirectory by default, so it doesn’t automatically share configuration, cached\\nauth, or session history with the Windows app.\\n\\nTo share them, use one of these approaches:\\n\\n- Sync WSL `~/.codex` with `%USERPROFILE%\\\\.codex` on your file system.\\n- Point WSL at the Windows Codex home directory by setting `CODEX_HOME`:\\n\\n```\\nexport CODEX_HOME=/mnt/c/Users/<windows-user>/.codex\\n```\\n\\nIf you want that setting in every shell, add it to your WSL shell profile, such\\nas `~/.bashrc` or `~/.zshrc`.\\n\\n### Git features are unavailable\\n\\nIf you don’t have Git installed natively on Windows, the app can’t use some\\nfeatures. Install it with `winget install Git.Git` from PowerShell or `cmd.exe`.\\n\\n### Git isn’t detected for projects opened from `\\\\\\\\wsl$`\\n\\nFor now, if you want to use the Windows-native agent with a project also\\naccessible from WSL, the most reliable workaround is to store the project\\non the native Windows drive and access it in WSL through `/mnt/<drive>/...`.\\n\\n### `Cmder` isn’t listed in the open dialog\\n\\nIf `Cmder` is installed but doesn’t show in Codex’s open dialog, add it to the\\nWindows Start Menu: right-click `Cmder` and choose __Add to Start__, then\\nrestart Codex or reboot.\",\"metadata\":{\"og:image\":\"https://developers.openai.com/og/codex/app/windows.png\",\"og:type\":\"website\",\"twitter:card\":\"summary_large_image\",\"twitter:title\":\"Windows – Codex app | OpenAI Developers\",\"twitter:url\":\"https://developers.openai.com/codex/app/windows\",\"og:title\":\"Windows – Codex app | OpenAI Developers\",\"description\":\"Use the Codex app on Windows with native sandbox and PowerShell support\",\"generator\":\"Astro v6.0.4\",\"title\":\"Windows – Codex app | OpenAI Developers\",\"og:description\":\"Use the Codex app on Windows with native sandbox and PowerShell support\",\"twitter:image\":\"https://developers.openai.com/og/codex/app/windows.png\",\"twitter:image:alt\":\"Windows – Codex app | OpenAI Developers\",\"twitter:site\":\"@OpenAIDevs\",\"viewport\":\"width=device-width,initial-scale=1\",\"twitter:description\":\"Use the Codex app on Windows with native sandbox and PowerShell support\",\"lang\":\"en\",\"og:url\":\"https://developers.openai.com/codex/app/windows\"},\"external\":{\"stylesheet\":{\"/_astro/PageLayout.CTPmIqDd.css\":{},\"/_astro/index.avIh2ZpK.css\":{}},\"icon\":{\"/favicon.png\":{\"type\":\"image/png\"}},\"alternate\":{\"https://developers.openai.com/rss.xml\":{\"title\":\"Windows ### Q10

"{\"title\":\"Advanced Configuration – Codex | OpenAI Developers\",\"description\":\"More advanced configuration options for Codex local clients\",\"url\":\"https://developers.openai.com/codex/config-advanced\",\"content\":\"Use these options when you need more control over providers, policies, and integrations. For a quick start, see Config basics.\\n\\nFor background on project guidance, reusable capabilities, custom slash commands, subagent workflows, and integrations, see Customization. For configuration keys, see Configuration Reference.\\n\\nProfiles let you save named sets of configuration values and switch between them from the CLI.\\n\\nProfiles are experimental and may change or be removed in future releases.\\n\\nProfiles are not currently supported in the Codex IDE extension.\\n\\nDefine profiles under `[profiles.<name>]` in `config.toml`, then run `codex --profile <name>`:\\n\\n```\\nmodel = \\\"gpt-5.4\\\"\\napproval_policy = \\\"on-request\\\"\\nmodel_catalog_json = \\\"/Users/me/.codex/model-catalogs/default.json\\\"\\n\\n[profiles.deep-review]\\nmodel = \\\"gpt-5-pro\\\"\\nmodel_reasoning_effort = \\\"high\\\"\\napproval_policy = \\\"never\\\"\\nmodel_catalog_json = \\\"/Users/me/.codex/model-catalogs/deep-review.json\\\"\\n\\n[profiles.lightweight]\\nmodel = \\\"gpt-4.1\\\"\\napproval_policy = \\\"untrusted\\\"\\n```\\n\\nTo make a profile the default, add `profile = \\\"deep-review\\\"` at the top level of `config.toml`. Codex loads that profile unless you override it on the command line.\\n\\nProfiles can also override `model_catalog_json`. When both the top level and the selected profile set `model_catalog_json`, Codex prefers the profile value.\\n\\nIn addition to editing `~/.codex/config.toml`, you can override configuration for a single run from the CLI:\\n\\n- Prefer dedicated flags when they exist (for example, `--model`).\\n- Use `-c` / `--config` when you need to override an arbitrary key.\\n\\nExamples:\\n\\n```\\n# Dedicated flag\\ncodex --model gpt-5.4\\n\\n# Generic key/value override (value is TOML, not JSON)\\ncodex --config model='\\\"gpt-5.4\\\"'\\ncodex --config sandbox_workspace_write.network_access=true\\ncodex --config 'shell_environment_policy.include_only=[\\\"PATH\\\",\\\"HOME\\\"]'\\n```\\n\\nNotes:\\n\\n- Keys can use dot notation to set nested values (for example, `mcp_servers.context7.enabled=false`).\\n- `--config` values are parsed as TOML. When in doubt, quote the value so your shell doesn’t split it on spaces.\\n- If the value can’t be parsed as TOML, Codex treats it as a string.\\n\\nCodex stores its local state under `CODEX_HOME` (defaults to `~/.codex`).\\n\\nCommon files you may see there:\\n\\n- `config.toml` (your local configuration)\\n- `auth.json` (if you use file-based credential storage) or your OS keychain/keyring\\n- `history.jsonl` (if history persistence is enabled)\\n- Other per-user state such as logs and caches\\n\\nFor authentication details (including credential storage modes), see Authentication. For the full list of configuration keys, see Configuration Reference.\\n\\nFor shared defaults, rules, and skills checked into repos or system paths, see Team Config.\\n\\nIf you just need to point the built-in OpenAI provider at an LLM proxy, router, or data-residency enabled project, set `openai_base_url` in `config.toml` instead of defining a new provider. This changes the base URL for the built-in `openai` provider without requiring a separate `model_providers.<id>` entry.\\n\\n```\\nopenai_base_url = \\\"https://us.api.openai.com/v1\\\"\\n```\\n\\nIn addition to your user config, Codex reads project-scoped overrides from `.codex/config.toml` files inside your repo. Codex walks from the project root to your current working directory and loads every `.codex/config.toml` it finds. If multiple files define the same key, the closest file to your working directory wins.\\n\\nFor security, Codex loads project-scoped config files only when the project is trusted. If the project is untrusted, Codex ignores project `.codex/` layers, including `.codex/config.toml`, project-local hooks, and project-local rules. User and system layers remain separate and still load.\\n\\nRelative paths inside a project config (for example, `model_instructions_file`) are resolved relative to the `.codex/` folder that contains the `config.toml`.\\n\\nProject config files can’t override settings that redirect credentials, change\\nprovider auth, or run machine-local notification/telemetry commands.\\nCodex ignores the following keys in project-local `.codex/config.toml` and\\nprints a startup warning when it sees them: `openai_base_url`,\\n`chatgpt_base_url`, `model_provider`, `model_providers`, `notify`, `profile`,\\n`profiles`, `experimental_realtime_ws_base_url`, and `otel`. Set those keys in\\nyour user-level `~/.codex/config.toml` instead.\\n\\nCodex can also load lifecycle hooks from either `hooks.json` files or inline\\n`[hooks]` tables in `config.toml` files that sit next to active config layers.\\n\\nIn practice, the four most useful locations are:\\n\\n- `~/.codex/hooks.json`\\n- `~/.codex/config.toml`\\n- `<repo>/.codex/hooks.json`\\n- `<repo>/.codex/config.toml`\\n\\nProject-local hooks load only when the project `.codex/` layer is trusted.\\nUser-level hooks remain independent of project trust.\\n\\nInline TOML hooks use the same event structure as `hooks.json`:\\n\\n```\\n[[hooks.PreToolUse]]\\nmatcher = \\\"^Bash$\\\"\\n\\n[[hooks.PreToolUse.hooks]]\\ntype = \\\"command\\\"\\ncommand = '/usr/bin/python3 \\\"$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)/.codex/hooks/pre_tool_use_policy.py\\\"'\\ntimeout = 30\\nstatusMessage = \\\"Checking Bash command\\\"\\n```\\n\\nIf a single layer contains both `hooks.json` and inline `[hooks]`, Codex loads\\nboth and warns. Prefer one representation per layer.\\n\\nFor the current event list, input fields, output behavior, and limitations, see\\nHooks.\\n\\nFor subagent role configuration (`[agents]` in `config.toml`), see Subagents.\\n\\nCodex discovers project configuration (for example, `.codex/` layers and `AGENTS.md`) by walking up from the working directory until it reaches a project root.\\n\\nBy default, Codex treats a directory containing `.git` as the project root. To customize this behavior, set `project_root_markers` in `config.toml`:\\n\\n```\\n# Treat a directory as the project root when it contains any of these markers.\\nproject_root_markers = [\\\".git\\\", \\\".hg\\\", \\\".sl\\\"]\\n```\\n\\nSet `project_root_markers = []` to skip searching parent directories and treat the current working directory as the project root.\\n\\nA model provider defines how Codex connects to a model (base URL, wire API, authentication, and optional HTTP headers). Custom providers can’t reuse the reserved built-in provider IDs: `openai`, `ollama`, and `lmstudio`.\\n\\nDefine additional providers and point `model_provider` at them:\\n\\n```\\nmodel = \\\"gpt-5.4\\\"\\nmodel_provider = \\\"proxy\\\"\\n\\n[model_providers.proxy]\\nname = \\\"OpenAI using LLM proxy\\\"\\nbase_url = \\\"http://proxy.example.com\\\"\\nenv_key = \\\"OPENAI_API_KEY\\\"\\n\\n[model_providers.local_ollama]\\nname = \\\"Ollama\\\"\\nbase_url = \\\"http://localhost:11434/v1\\\"\\n\\n[model_providers.mistral]\\nname = \\\"Mistral\\\"\\nbase_url = \\\"https://api.mistral.ai/v1\\\"\\nenv_key = \\\"MISTRAL_API_KEY\\\"\\n```\\n\\nAdd request headers when needed:\\n\\n```\\n[model_providers.example]\\nhttp_headers = { \\\"X-Example-Header\\\" = \\\"example-value\\\" }\\nenv_http_headers = { \\\"X-Example-Features\\\" = \\\"EXAMPLE_FEATURES\\\" }\\n```\\n\\nUse command-backed authentication when a provider needs Codex to fetch bearer tokens from an external credential helper:\\n\\n```\\n[model_providers.proxy]\\nname = \\\"OpenAI using LLM proxy\\\"\\nbase_url = \\\"https://proxy.example.com/v1\\\"\\nwire_api = \\\"responses\\\"\\n\\n[model_providers.proxy.auth]\\ncommand = \\\"/usr/local/bin/fetch-codex-token\\\"\\nargs = [\\\"--audience\\\", \\\"codex\\\"]\\ntimeout_ms = 5000\\nrefresh_interval_ms = 300000\\n```\\n\\nThe auth command receives no `stdin` and must print the token to stdout. Codex trims surrounding whitespace, treats an empty token as an error, and refreshes proactively at `refresh_interval_ms`; set `refresh_interval_ms = 0` to refresh only after an authentication retry. Don’t combine `[model_providers.<id>.auth]` with `env_key`, `experimental_bearer_token`, or `requires_openai_auth`.\\n\\n### Amazon Bedrock provider\\n\\nCodex includes a built-in `amazon-bedrock` model provider. Set it directly as\\n`model_provider`; unlike custom providers, this built-in provider supports only\\nthe nested AWS profile and region overrides.\\n\\n```\\nmodel_provider = \\\"amazon-bedrock\\\"\\nmodel = \\\"<bedrock-model-id>\\\"\\n\\n[model_providers.amazon-bedrock.aws]\\nprofile = \\\"default\\\"\\nregion = \\\"eu-central-1\\\"\\n```\\n\\nIf you omit `profile`, Codex uses the standard AWS credential chain. Set\\n`region` to the supported Bedrock region that should handle requests.\\n\\nCodex can run against a local “open source” provider (for example, Ollama or LM Studio) when you pass `--oss`. If you pass `--oss` without specifying a provider, Codex uses `oss_provider` as the default.\\n\\n```\\n# Default local provider used with `--oss`\\noss_provider = \\\"ollama\\\" # or \\\"lmstudio\\\"\\n```\\n\\n```\\n[model_providers.azure]\\nname = \\\"Azure\\\"\\nbase_url = \\\"https://YOUR_PROJECT_NAME.openai.azure.com/openai\\\"\\nenv_key = \\\"AZURE_OPENAI_API_KEY\\\"\\nquery_params = { api-version = \\\"2025-04-01-preview\\\" }\\nwire_api = \\\"responses\\\"\\nrequest_max_retries = 4\\nstream_max_retries = 10\\nstream_idle_timeout_ms = 300000\\n```\\n\\nTo change the base URL for the built-in OpenAI provider, use `openai_base_url`; don’t create `[model_providers.openai]`, because you can’t override built-in provider IDs.\\n\\nProjects created with data residency enabled can create a model provider to update the base_url with the correct prefix.\\n\\n```\\nmodel_provider = \\\"openaidr\\\"\\n[model_providers.openaidr]\\nname = \\\"OpenAI Data Residency\\\"\\nbase_url = \\\"https://us.api.openai.com/v1\\\" # Replace 'us' with domain prefix\\n```\\n\\n```\\nmodel_reasoning_summary = \\\"none\\\"          # Disable summaries\\nmodel_verbosity = \\\"low\\\"                   # Shorten responses\\nmodel_supports_reasoning_summaries = true # Force reasoning\\nmodel_context_window = 128000             # Context window size\\n```\\n\\n`model_verbosity` applies only to providers using the Responses API. Chat Completions providers will ignore the setting.\\n\\nPick approval strictness (affects when Codex pauses) and sandbox level (affects file/network access).\\n\\nFor operational details to keep in mind while editing `config.toml`, see Common sandbox and approval combinations, Protected paths in writable roots, and Network access.\\n\\nFor beta permission profiles that configure filesystem and network access together, see Permissions.\\n\\nYou can also use a granular approval policy (`approval_policy = { granular = { ... } }`) to allow or auto-reject individual prompt categories. This is useful when you want normal interactive approvals for some cases but want others, such as `request_permissions` or skill-script prompts, to fail closed automatically.\\n\\nSet `approvals_reviewer = \\\"auto_review\\\"` to route eligible interactive approval\\nrequests through automatic review. This changes the reviewer, not the sandbox\\nboundary.\\n\\nUse `[auto_review].policy` for local reviewer policy instructions. Managed\\n`guardian_policy_config` takes precedence.\\n\\n```\\napproval_policy = \\\"untrusted\\\"   # Other options: on-request, never, or { granular = { ... } }\\napprovals_reviewer = \\\"user\\\"     # Or \\\"auto_review\\\" for automatic review\\nsandbox_mode = \\\"workspace-write\\\"\\nallow_login_shell = false       # Optional hardening: disallow login shells for shell tools\\n\\n# Example granular approval policy:\\n# approval_policy = { granular = {\\n#   sandbox_approval = true,\\n#   rules = true,\\n#   mcp_elicitations = true,\\n#   request_permissions = false,\\n#   skill_approval = false\\n# } }\\n\\n[sandbox_workspace_write]\\nexclude_tmpdir_env_var = false  # Allow $TMPDIR\\nexclude_slash_tmp = false       # Allow /tmp\\nwritable_roots = [\\\"/Users/YOU/.pyenv/shims\\\"]\\nnetwork_access = false          # Opt in to outbound network\\n\\n[auto_review]\\npolicy = \\\"\\\"\\\"\\nUse your organization's automatic review policy.\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n```\\n\\n### Named permission profiles\\n\\nFor built-in profiles, custom profile syntax, and the full filesystem and\\nnetwork configuration model, see Permissions.\\n\\nFor the complete key list, including profile-scoped overrides and requirements\\nconstraints, see Configuration Reference and\\nManaged configuration.\\n\\nIn workspace-write mode, some environments keep `.git/` and `.codex/`\\nread-only even when the rest of the workspace is writable. This is why\\ncommands like `git commit` may still require approval to run outside the\\nsandbox. If you want Codex to skip specific commands (for example, block `git commit` outside the sandbox), use\\nrules.\\n\\nDisable sandboxing entirely (use only if your environment already isolates processes):\\n\\n```\\nsandbox_mode = \\\"danger-full-access\\\"\\n```\\n\\n`shell_environment_policy` controls which environment variables Codex passes to any subprocess it launches (for example, when running a tool-command the model proposes). Start from a clean start (`inherit = \\\"none\\\"`) or a trimmed set (`inherit = \\\"core\\\"`), then layer on excludes, includes, and overrides to avoid leaking secrets while still providing the paths, keys, or flags your tasks need.\\n\\n```\\n[shell_environment_policy]\\ninherit = \\\"none\\\"\\nset = { PATH = \\\"/usr/bin\\\", MY_FLAG = \\\"1\\\" }\\nignore_default_excludes = false\\nexclude = [\\\"AWS_*\\\", \\\"AZURE_*\\\"]\\ninclude_only = [\\\"PATH\\\", \\\"HOME\\\"]\\n```\\n\\nPatterns are case-insensitive globs (`*`, `?`, `[A-Z]`); `ignore_default_excludes = false` keeps the automatic KEY/SECRET/TOKEN filter before your includes/excludes run.\\n\\nSee the dedicated MCP documentation for configuration details.\\n\\nEnable OpenTelemetry (OTel) log export to track Codex runs (API requests, SSE/events, prompts, tool approvals/results). Disabled by default; opt in via `[otel]`:\\n\\n```\\n[otel]\\nenvironment = \\\"staging\\\"   # defaults to \\\"dev\\\"\\nexporter = \\\"none\\\"         # set to otlp-http or otlp-grpc to send events\\nlog_user_prompt = false   # redact user prompts unless explicitly enabled\\n```\\n\\nChoose an exporter:\\n\\n```\\n[otel]\\nexporter = { otlp-http = {\\n  endpoint = \\\"https://otel.example.com/v1/logs\\\",\\n  protocol = \\\"binary\\\",\\n  headers = { \\\"x-otlp-api-key\\\" = \\\"${OTLP_TOKEN}\\\" }\\n}}\\n```\\n\\n```\\n[otel]\\nexporter = { otlp-grpc = {\\n  endpoint = \\\"https://otel.example.com:4317\\\",\\n  headers = { \\\"x-otlp-meta\\\" = \\\"abc123\\\" }\\n}}\\n```\\n\\nIf `exporter = \\\"none\\\"` Codex records events but sends nothing. Exporters batch asynchronously and flush on shutdown. Event metadata includes service name, CLI version, env tag, conversation id, model, sandbox/approval settings, and per-event fields (see Config Reference).\\n\\n### What gets emitted\\n\\nCodex emits structured log events for runs and tool usage. Representative event types include:\\n\\n- `codex.conversation_starts` (model, reasoning settings, sandbox/approval policy)\\n- `codex.api_request` (attempt, status/success, duration, and error details)\\n- `codex.sse_event` (stream event kind, success/failure, duration, plus token counts on `response.completed`)\\n- `codex.websocket_request` and `codex.websocket_event` (request duration plus per-message kind/success/error)\\n- `codex.user_prompt` (length; content redacted unless explicitly enabled)\\n- `codex.tool_decision` (approved/denied and whether the decision came from config vs user)\\n- `codex.tool_result` (duration, success, output snippet)\\n\\n### OTel metrics emitted\\n\\nWhen the OTel metrics pipeline is enabled, Codex emits counters and duration histograms for API, stream, and tool activity.\\n\\nEach metric below also includes default metadata tags: `auth_mode`, `originator`, `session_source`, `model`, and `app.version`.\\n\\n| Metric | Type | Fields | Description |\\n| --- | --- | --- | --- |\\n| `codex.api_request` | counter | `status`, `success` | API request count by HTTP status and success/failure. |\\n| `codex.api_request.duration_ms` | histogram | `status`, `success` | API request duration in milliseconds. |\\n| `codex.sse_event` | counter | `kind`, `success` | SSE event count by event kind and success/failure. |\\n| `codex.sse_event.duration_ms` | histogram | `kind`, `success` | SSE event processing duration in milliseconds. |\\n| `codex.websocket.request` | counter | `success` | WebSocket request count by success/failure. |\\n| `codex.websocket.request.duration_ms` | histogram | `success` | WebSocket request duration in milliseconds. |\\n| `codex.websocket.event` | counter | `kind`, `success` | WebSocket message/event count by type and success/failure. |\\n| `codex.websocket.event.duration_ms` | histogram | `kind`, `success` | WebSocket message/event processing duration in milliseconds. |\\n| `codex.tool.call` | counter | `tool`, `success` | Tool invocation count by tool name and success/failure. |\\n| `codex.tool.call.duration_ms` | histogram | `tool`, `success` | Tool execution duration in milliseconds by tool name and outcome. |\\n\\nFor more security and privacy guidance around telemetry, see Security.\\n\\n### Metrics\\n\\nBy default, Codex periodically sends a small amount of anonymous usage and health data back to OpenAI. This helps detect when Codex isn’t working correctly and shows what features and configuration options are being used, so the Codex team can focus on what matters most. These metrics don’t contain any personally identifiable information (PII). Metrics collection is independent of OTel log/trace export.\\n\\nIf you want to disable metrics collection entirely across Codex surfaces on a machine, set the analytics flag in your config:\\n\\n```\\n[analytics]\\nenabled = false\\n```\\n\\nEach metric includes its own fields plus the default context fields below.\\n\\n#### Default context fields (applies to every event/metric)\\n\\n- `auth_mode`: `swic` | `api` | `unknown`.\\n- `model`: name of the model used.\\n- `app.version`: Codex version.\\n\\n#### Metrics catalog\\n\\nEach metric includes the required fields plus the default context fields above. Metric names below omit the `codex.` prefix.\\nMost metric names are centralized in `codex-rs/otel/src/metrics/names.rs`; feature-specific metrics emitted outside that file are included here too.\\nIf a metric includes the `tool` field, it reflects the internal tool used (for example, `apply_patch` or `shell`) and doesn’t contain the actual shell command or patch `codex` is trying to apply.\\n\\n#### Runtime and model transport\\n\\n| Metric | Type | Fields | Description |\\n| --- | --- | --- | --- |\\n| `api_request` | counter | `status`, `success` | API request count by HTTP status and success/failure. |\\n| `api_request.duration_ms` | histogram | `status`, `success` | API request duration in milliseconds. |\\n| `sse_event` | counter | `kind`, `success` | SSE event count by event kind and success/failure. |\\n| `sse_event.duration_ms` | histogram | `kind`, `success` | SSE event processing duration in milliseconds. |\\n| `websocket.request` | counter | `success` | WebSocket request count by success/failure. |\\n| `websocket.request.duration_ms` | histogram | `success` | WebSocket request duration in milliseconds. |\\n| `websocket.event` | counter | `kind`, `success` | WebSocket message/event count by type and success/failure. |\\n| `websocket.event.duration_ms` | histogram | `kind`, `success` | WebSocket message/event processing duration in milliseconds. |\\n| `responses_api_overhead.duration_ms` | histogram |  | Responses API overhead timing from WebSocket responses. |\\n| `responses_api_inference_time.duration_ms` | histogram |  | Responses API inference timing from WebSocket responses. |\\n| `responses_api_engine_iapi_ttft.duration_ms` | histogram |  | Responses API engine IAPI time-to-first-token timing. |\\n| `responses_api_engine_service_ttft.duration_ms` | histogram |  | Responses API engine service time-to-first-token timing. |\\n| `responses_api_engine_iapi_tbt.duration_ms` | histogram |  | Responses API engine IAPI time-between-token timing. |\\n| `responses_api_engine_service_tbt.duration_ms` | histogram |  | Responses API engine service time-between-token timing. |\\n| `transport.fallback_to_http` | counter | `from_wire_api` | WebSocket-to-HTTP fallback count. |\\n| `remote_models.fetch_update.duration_ms` | histogram |  | Time to fetch remote model definitions. |\\n| `remote_models.load_cache.duration_ms` | histogram |  | Time to load the remote model cache. |\\n| `startup_prewarm.duration_ms` | histogram | `status` | Startup prewarm duration by outcome. |\\n| `startup_prewarm.age_at_first_turn_ms` | histogram | `status` | Startup prewarm age when the first real turn resolves it. |\\n| `cloud_requirements.fetch.duration_ms` | histogram |  | Workspace-managed cloud requirements fetch duration. |\\n| `cloud_requirements.fetch_attempt` | counter | See note | Workspace-managed cloud requirements fetch attempts. |\\n| `cloud_requirements.fetch_final` | counter | See note | Final workspace-managed cloud requirements fetch outcome. |\\n| `cloud_requirements.load` | counter | `trigger`, `outcome` | Workspace-managed cloud requirements load outcome. |\\n\\nThe `cloud_requirements.fetch_attempt` metric includes `trigger`, `attempt`, `outcome`, and `status_code` fields. The `cloud_requirements.fetch_final` metric includes `trigger`, `outcome`, `reason`, `attempt_count`, and `status_code` fields.\\n\\n#### Turn and tool activity\\n\\n| Metric | Type | Fields | Description |\\n| --- | --- | --- | --- |\\n| `turn.e2e_duration_ms` | histogram |  | End-to-end time for a full turn. |\\n| `turn.ttft.duration_ms` | histogram |  | Time to first token for a turn. |\\n| `turn.ttfm.duration_ms` | histogram |  | Time to first model output item for a turn. |\\n| `turn.network_proxy` | counter | `active`, `tmp_mem_enabled` | Whether the managed network proxy was active for the turn. |\\n| `turn.memory` | counter | `read_allowed`, `feature_enabled`, `config_use_memories`, `has_citations` | Per-turn memory read availability and memory citation usage. |\\n| `turn.tool.call` | histogram | `tmp_mem_enabled` | Number of tool calls in the turn. |\\n| `turn.token_usage` | histogram | `token_type`, `tmp_mem_enabled` | Per-turn token usage by token type (`total`, `input`, `cached_input`, `output`, or `reasoning_output`). |\\n| `tool.call` | counter | `tool`, `success` | Tool invocation count by tool name and success/failure. |\\n| `tool.call.duration_ms` | histogram | `tool`, `success` | Tool execution duration in milliseconds by tool name and outcome. |\\n| `tool.unified_exec` | counter | `tty` | Unified exec tool calls by TTY mode. |\\n| `approval.requested` | counter | `tool`, `approved` | Tool approval request result (`approved`, `approved_with_amendment`, `approved_for_session`, `denied`, `abort`). |\\n| `mcp.call` | counter | See note | MCP tool invocation result. |\\n| `mcp.call.duration_ms` | histogram | See note | MCP tool invocation duration. |\\n| `mcp.tools.list.duration_ms` | histogram | `cache` | MCP tool-list duration, including cache hit/miss state. |\\n| `mcp.tools.fetch_uncached.duration_ms` | histogram |  | Duration of MCP tool fetches that miss the cache. |\\n| `mcp.tools.cache_write.duration_ms` | histogram |  | Duration of Codex Apps MCP tool-cache writes. |\\n| `hooks.run` | counter | `hook_name`, `source`, `status` | Hook run count by hook name, source, and status. |\\n| `hooks.run.duration_ms` | histogram | `hook_name`, `source`, `status` | Hook run duration in milliseconds. |\\n\\nThe `mcp.call` and `mcp.call.duration_ms` metrics include `status`; normal tool-call emissions also include `tool`, plus `connector_id` and `connector_name` when available. Blocked Codex Apps MCP calls may emit `mcp.call` with only `status`.\\n\\n#### Threads, tasks, and features\\n\\n| Metric | Type | Fields | Description |\\n| --- | --- | --- | --- |\\n| `feature.state` | counter | `feature`, `value` | Feature values that differ from defaults (emit one row per non-default). |\\n| `status_line` | counter |  | Session started with a configured status line. |\\n| `model_warning` | counter |  | Warning sent to the model. |\\n| `thread.started` | counter | `is_git` | New thread created, tagged by whether the working directory is in a Git repo. |\\n| `conversation.turn.count` | counter |  | User/assistant turns per thread, recorded at the end of the thread. |\\n| `thread.fork` | counter | `source` | New thread created by forking an existing thread. |\\n| `thread.rename` | counter |  | Thread renamed. |\\n| `thread.side` | counter | `source` | Side conversation created. |\\n| `thread.skills.enabled_total` | histogram |  | Number of skills enabled for a new thread. |\\n| `thread.skills.kept_total` | histogram |  | Number of enabled skills kept after prompt rendering. |\\n| `thread.skills.truncated` | histogram |  | Whether skill rendering truncated the enabled skills list (`1` or `0`). |\\n| `task.compact` | counter | `type` | Number of compactions per type (`remote` or `local`), including manual and auto. |\\n| `task.review` | counter |  | Number of reviews triggered. |\\n| `task.undo` | counter |  | Number of undo actions triggered. |\\n| `task.user_shell` | counter |  | Number of user shell actions (`!` in the TUI for example). |\\n| `shell_snapshot` | counter | See note | Whether taking a shell snapshot succeeded. |\\n| `shell_snapshot.duration_ms` | histogram | `success` | Time to take a shell snapshot. |\\n| `skill.injected` | counter | `status`, `skill` | Skill injection outcomes by skill. |\\n| `plugins.startup_sync` | counter | `transport`, `status` | Curated plugin startup sync attempts. |\\n| `plugins.startup_sync.final` | counter | `transport`, `status` | Final curated plugin startup sync outcome. |\\n| `multi_agent.spawn` | counter | `role` | Agent spawns by role. |\\n| `multi_agent.resume` | counter |  | Agent resumes. |\\n| `multi_agent.nickname_pool_reset` | counter |  | Agent nickname pool resets. |\\n\\nThe `shell_snapshot` metric includes `success` and, on failures, `failure_reason`.\\n\\n#### Memory and local state\\n\\n| Metric | Type | Fields | Description |\\n| --- | --- | --- | --- |\\n| `memory.phase1` | counter | `status` | Memory phase 1 job counts by status. |\\n| `memory.phase1.e2e_ms` | histogram |  | End-to-end duration for memory phase 1. |\\n| `memory.phase1.output` | counter |  | Memory phase 1 outputs written. |\\n| `memory.phase1.token_usage` | histogram | `token_type` | Memory phase 1 token usage by token type. |\\n| `memory.phase2` | counter | `status` | Memory phase 2 job counts by status. |\\n| `memory.phase2.e2e_ms` | histogram |  | End-to-end duration for memory phase 2. |\\n| `memory.phase2.input` | counter |  | Memory phase 2 input count. |\\n| `memory.phase2.token_usage` | histogram | `token_type` | Memory phase 2 token usage by token type. |\\n| `memories.usage` | counter | `kind`, `tool`, `success` | Memory usage by kind, tool, and success/failure. |\\n| `external_agent_config.detect` | counter | See note | External agent config detections by migration item type. |\\n| `external_agent_config.import` | counter | See note | External agent config imports by migration item type. |\\n| `db.backfill` | counter | `status` | Initial state DB backfill results (`upserted`, `failed`). |\\n| `db.backfill.duration_ms` | histogram | `status` | Duration of the initial state DB backfill. |\\n| `db.error` | counter | `stage` | Errors during state DB operations. |\\n\\nThe `external_agent_config.detect` and `external_agent_config.import` metrics include `migration_type`; skills migrations also include `skills_count`.\\n\\n#### Windows sandbox\\n\\n| Metric | Type | Fields | Description |\\n| --- | --- | --- | --- |\\n| `windows_sandbox.setup_success` | counter | `originator`, `mode` | Windows sandbox setup successes. |\\n| `windows_sandbox.setup_failure` | counter | `originator`, `mode` | Windows sandbox setup failures. |\\n| `windows_sandbox.setup_duration_ms` | histogram | `result`, `originator`, `mode` | Windows sandbox setup duration. |\\n| `windows_sandbox.elevated_setup_success` | counter |  | Elevated Windows sandbox setup successes. |\\n| `windows_sandbox.elevated_setup_failure` | counter | See note | Elevated Windows sandbox setup failures. |\\n| `windows_sandbox.elevated_setup_canceled` | counter | See note | Canceled elevated Windows sandbox setup attempts. |\\n| `windows_sandbox.elevated_setup_duration_ms` | histogram | `result` | Elevated Windows sandbox setup duration. |\\n| `windows_sandbox.elevated_prompt_shown` | counter |  | Elevated sandbox setup prompt shown. |\\n| `windows_sandbox.elevated_prompt_accept` | counter |  | Elevated sandbox setup prompt accepted. |\\n| `windows_sandbox.elevated_prompt_use_legacy` | counter |  | User chose legacy sandbox from the elevated prompt. |\\n| `windows_sandbox.elevated_prompt_quit` | counter |  | User quit from the elevated prompt. |\\n| `windows_sandbox.fallback_prompt_shown` | counter |  | Fallback sandbox prompt shown. |\\n| `windows_sandbox.fallback_retry_elevated` | counter |  | User retried elevated setup from the fallback prompt. |\\n| `windows_sandbox.fallback_use_legacy` | counter |  | User chose legacy sandbox from the fallback prompt. |\\n| `windows_sandbox.fallback_prompt_quit` | counter |  | User quit from the fallback prompt. |\\n| `windows_sandbox.legacy_setup_preflight_failed` | counter | See note | Legacy Windows sandbox setup preflight failure. |\\n| `windows_sandbox.setup_elevated_sandbox_command` | counter |  | Elevated sandbox setup command invoked. |\\n| `windows_sandbox.createprocessasuserw_failed` | counter | `error_code`, `path_kind`, `exe`, `level` | Windows `CreateProcessAsUserW` failures. |\\n\\nThe elevated setup failure metrics include `code` and `message` when Windows setup failure details are available, and may include `originator` when emitted from the shared setup path. The `windows_sandbox.legacy_setup_preflight_failed` metric includes `originator` when emitted from the shared setup path, but fallback-prompt preflight failures may not include any fields.\\n\\n### Feedback controls\\n\\nBy default, Codex lets users send feedback from `/feedback`. To disable feedback collection across Codex surfaces on a machine, update your config:\\n\\n```\\n[feedback]\\nenabled = false\\n```\\n\\nWhen disabled, `/feedback` shows a disabled message and Codex rejects feedback submissions.\\n\\n### Hide or surface reasoning events\\n\\nIf you want to reduce noisy “reasoning” output (for example in CI logs), you can suppress it:\\n\\n```\\nhide_agent_reasoning = true\\n```\\n\\nIf you want to surface raw reasoning content when a model emits it:\\n\\n```\\nshow_raw_agent_reasoning = true\\n```\\n\\nEnable raw reasoning only if it’s acceptable for your workflow. Some models/providers (like `gpt-oss`) don’t emit raw reasoning; in that case, this setting has no visible effect.\\n\\nUse `notify` to trigger an external program whenever Codex emits supported events (currently only `agent-turn-complete`). This is handy for desktop toasts, chat webhooks, CI updates, or any side-channel alerting that the built-in TUI notifications don’t cover.\\n\\n```\\nnotify = [\\\"python3\\\", \\\"/path/to/notify.py\\\"]\\n```\\n\\nExample `notify.py` (truncated) that reacts to `agent-turn-complete`:\\n\\n```\\n#!/usr/bin/env python3\\nimport json, subprocess, sys\\n\\ndef main() -> int:\\n    notification = json.loads(sys.argv[1])\\n    if notification.get(\\\"type\\\") != \\\"agent-turn-complete\\\":\\n        return 0\\n    title = f\\\"Codex: {notification.get('last-assistant-message', 'Turn Complete!')}\\\"\\n    message = \\\" \\\".join(notification.get(\\\"input-messages\\\", []))\\n    subprocess.check_output([\\n        \\\"terminal-notifier\\\",\\n        \\\"-title\\\", title,\\n        \\\"-message\\\", message,\\n        \\\"-group\\\", \\\"codex-\\\" + notification.get(\\\"thread-id\\\", \\\"\\\"),\\n        \\\"-activate\\\", \\\"com.googlecode.iterm2\\\",\\n    ])\\n    return 0\\n\\nif __name__ == \\\"__main__\\\":\\n    sys.exit(main())\\n```\\n\\nThe script receives a single JSON argument. Common fields include:\\n\\n- `type` (currently `agent-turn-complete`)\\n- `thread-id` (session identifier)\\n- `turn-id` (turn identifier)\\n- `cwd` (working directory)\\n- `input-messages` (user messages that led to the turn)\\n- `last-assistant-message` (last assistant message text)\\n\\nPlace the script somewhere on disk and point `notify` to it.\\n\\n#### `notify` vs `tui.notifications`\\n\\n- `notify` runs an external program (good for webhooks, desktop notifiers, CI hooks).\\n- `tui.notifications` is built in to the TUI and can optionally filter by event type (for example, `agent-turn-complete` and `approval-requested`).\\n- `tui.notification_method` controls how the TUI emits terminal notifications (`auto`, `osc9`, or `bel`).\\n- `tui.notification_condition` controls whether TUI notifications fire only when\\n  the terminal is `unfocused` or `always`.\\n\\nIn `auto` mode, Codex prefers OSC 9 notifications (a terminal escape sequence some terminals interpret as a desktop notification) and falls back to BEL (`\\\\x07`) otherwise.\\n\\nSee Configuration Reference for the exact keys.\\n\\n## History persistence\\n\\nBy default, Codex saves local session transcripts under `CODEX_HOME` (for example, `~/.codex/history.jsonl`). To disable local history persistence:\\n\\n```\\n[history]\\npersistence = \\\"none\\\"\\n```\\n\\nTo cap the history file size, set `history.max_bytes`. When the file exceeds the cap, Codex drops the oldest entries and compacts the file while keeping the newest records.\\n\\n```\\n[history]\\nmax_bytes = 104857600 # 100 MiB\\n```\\n\\nIf you use a terminal/editor integration that supports it, Codex can render file citations as clickable links. Configure `file_opener` to pick the URI scheme Codex uses:\\n\\n```\\nfile_opener = \\\"vscode\\\" # or cursor, windsurf, vscode-insiders, none\\n```\\n\\nExample: a citation like `/home/user/project/main.py:42` can be rewritten into a clickable `vscode://file/...:42` link.\\n\\nCodex reads `AGENTS.md` (and related files) and includes a limited amount of project guidance in the first turn of a session. Two knobs control how this works:\\n\\n- `project_doc_max_bytes`: how much to read from each `AGENTS.md` file\\n- `project_doc_fallback_filenames`: additional filenames to try when `AGENTS.md` is missing at a directory level\\n\\nFor a detailed walkthrough, see Custom instructions with AGENTS.md.\\n\\nRunning `codex` with no subcommand launches the interactive terminal UI (TUI). Codex exposes some TUI-specific configuration under `[tui]`, including:\\n\\n- `tui.notifications`: enable/disable notifications (or restrict to specific types)\\n- `tui.notification_method`: choose `auto`, `osc9`, or `bel` for terminal notifications\\n- `tui.notification_condition`: choose `unfocused` or `always` for when\\n  notifications fire\\n- `tui.animations`: enable/disable ASCII animations and shimmer effects\\n- `tui.alternate_screen`: control alternate screen usage (set to `never` to keep terminal scrollback)\\n- `tui.show_tooltips`: show or hide onboarding tooltips on the welcome screen\\n\\n`tui.notification_method` defaults to `auto`. In `auto` mode, Codex prefers OSC 9 notifications (a terminal escape sequence some terminals interpret as a desktop notification) when the terminal appears to support them, and falls back to BEL (`\\\\x07`) otherwise.\\n\\nSee Configuration Reference for the full key list.\",\"metadata\":{\"og:image\":\"https://developers.openai.com/og/codex/config-advanced.png\",\"og:type\":\"website\",\"twitter:card\":\"summary_large_image\",\"twitter:title\":\"Advanced Configuration – Codex | OpenAI Developers\",\"twitter:url\":\"https://developers.openai.com/codex/config-advanced\",\"og:title\":\"Advanced Configuration – Codex | OpenAI Developers\",\"description\":\"More advanced configuration options for Codex local clients\",\"generator\":\"Astro v6.0.4\",\"title\":\"Advanced Configuration – Codex | OpenAI Developers\",\"og:description\":\"More advanced configuration options for Codex local clients\",\"twitter:image\":\"https://developers.openai.com/og/codex/config-advanced.png\",\"twitter:image:alt\":\"Advanced Configuration – Codex | OpenAI Developers\",\"twitter:site\":\"@OpenAIDevs\",\"viewport\":\"width=device-width,initial-scale=1\",\"twitter:description\":\"More advanced configuration options for Codex local clients\",\"lang\":\"en\",\"og:url\":\"https://developers.openai.com/codex/config-advanced\"},\"external\":{\"stylesheet\":{\"/_astro/PageLayout.Dgvfsk_l.css\":{},\"/_astro/index.avIh2ZpK.css\":{}},\"icon\":{\"/favicon.png\":{\"type\":\"image/png\"}},\"alternate\":{\"https://developers.openai.com/rss.xml\":{\"title\":\"Advanced Configuration – Codex | OpenAI Developers\",\"type\":\"application/rss+xml\"}},\"canonical\":{\"https://developers.openai.com/codex/config-advanced\":{}},\"preload\":{\"/fonts/OpenAISans-Bold.woff\":{\"type\":\"font/woff\"},\"/fonts/OpenAISans-Regular.woff\":{\"type\":\"font/woff\"}},\"sitemap\":{\"/sitemap-index.xml\":{}}}}"

### Q13

-rwxr-xr-x  1 tonyorz  staff  737  5 26 19:54 /Users/tonyorz/.local/bin/codex-zhipu-token
#!/bin/sh
set -eu

python3 <<'PY'
import json
import sys
from pathlib import Path

home = Path.home()
settings_path = home / ".claude" / "settings.json"
config_path = home / ".claude" / "config.json"

token = None

try:
    settings = json.loads(settings_path.read_text())
    token = settings.get("env", {}).get("ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN")
except Exception:
    token = None

if not token:
    try:
        config = json.loads(config_path.read_text())
        token = config.get("primaryApiKey")
    except Exception:
        token = None

token = (token or "").strip()
if not token:
    print("Could not find a Zhipu token in ~/.claude/settings.json or ~/.claude/config.json.", file=sys.stderr)
    sys.exit(1)

sys.stdout.write(token)
PY

Codex 是OpenAI 推出的AI 编程助手，支持CLI 命令行版和桌面App 版。两者共享同一套配置文件，本教程适用于macOS 和Windows。\",\"refer\":\"ref_2\"},{\"title\":\"Codex CLI：权威技术参考\",\"link\":\"https://blakecrosley.com/zh-Hans/guides/codex\",\"content\":\"Codex v0.133.0仍是最新稳定版CLI，同时Codex应用更新新增了Appshots、Goal模式正式发布、浏览器注释、锁定电脑使用、更强权限、插件发现、SDK认证和exec恢复架构。\",\"refer\":\"ref_3\"},{\"title\":\"Codex 保姆级入门教程（6000字长文） - 知乎专栏\",\"link\":\"https://zhuanlan.zhihu.com/p/2033918612927410238\",\"content\":\"跳过，后面再配置。 由于我之前登录过Codex CLI，所以App 这边不用登录就可以直接使用了。后面的IDE 插件也是一样的道理，同一套登录状态可以复用。 2 ...\",\"refer\":\"ref_4\"},{\"title\":\"使用自定义API接入OpenAI CodeX配置教程- 林恒 - 博客园\",\"link\":\"https://www.cnblogs.com/smileZAZ/p/19406570\",\"content\":\"安装后，我们开始配置codex才能通过apikey方式接入我们的API。如果你是通过openai plus会员来使用codex，可跳过后续教程，codex中直接登录plus账号即可使用。\",\"refer\":\"ref_5\"},{\"title\":\"Codex CLI 自定义模型使用指南\",\"link\":\"https://wener.me/notes/ai/service/codex-custom-model-guide\",\"content\":\"本指南专注于如何在Codex CLI 中配置和使用自定义模型，包括第三方API 服务、本地模型和自定义端点。 目录​. 快速开始; 模型提供程序配置; 常用第三方服务 ...\",\"refer\":\"ref_6\"},{\"title\":\"auth2api/README_CN.md at main - GitHub\",\"link\":\"https://github.com/AmazingAng/auth2api/blob/main/README_CN.md\",\"content\":\"一个轻量级OAuth 转API 代理，把你的Claude（Anthropic）、ChatGPT（OpenAI Codex）订阅，以及实验性的本地Cursor 登录态变成可调用的API，适配Claude Code 与OpenAI 兼容客户端。\",\"refer\":\"ref_7\"},{\"title\":\"[PDF] 大模型服务平台TokenHub Token Plan\",\"link\":\"https://main.qcloudimg.com/raw/document/product/pdf/1823_130059_cn.pdf\",\"content\":\"... 自定义模型. 单击右侧添加自定义模型，在弹出的添加模型对话框中，选择服务商为自定义，依次填写模型ID、API Key、. Base URL 等信息：. 配置Auto. Page ...\",\"refer\":\"ref_8\"},{\"title\":\"如何快速用上大模型：OpenAI Codex (OAuth) 配置教程\",\"link\":\"https://www.tencentcloud.com/techpedia/143915\",\"content\":\"2.1 步骤1：进入基础配置，选择模型. 登录并进入Lighthouse OpenClaw 管理面板; 点击左侧菜单栏中的「基础配置」; 找到第一步「模型(Models)」配置区域; 点击模型选择下拉框， ...\",\"refer\":\"ref_9\"},{\"title\":\"OpenAi Codex最佳API配置教程指南\",\"link\":\"https://api-gpt-ge.apifox.cn/7738964m0\",\"content\":\"安装后，我们开始配置codex才能通过apikey方式接入我们的API。如果你是通过openai plus会员来使用codex，可跳过后续教程，codex中直接登录plus账号即可使用。\",\"refer\":\"ref_10\"}]"

### Q24

"{\"title\":\"使用自定义API接入OpenAI CodeX配置教程\",\"description\":\"&#129489;‍&#128187; 写在开头 点赞 + 收藏 学会&#129315;&#129315;&#129315; Codex 是OpenAI 推出的一系列人工智能编码工具，通过将任务委托给强大的云端和本地编码代理，帮助开发人员提升工作效率。支持原生终端、vscode插件、cursor插件\",\"url\":\"https://www.cnblogs.com/smileZAZ/p/19406570\",\"content\":\"## 🧑‍💻 写在开头\\n\\n点赞 + 收藏 === 学会🤣🤣🤣\\n\\n> Codex 是OpenAI 推出的一系列人工智能编码工具，通过将任务委托给强大的云端和本地编码代理，帮助开发人员提升工作效率。支持原生终端、vscode插件、cursor插件等场景使用。\\n\\n官网网站 openai.com/codex/\\n\\n## 系统要求\\n\\n![Image 1: wechat_2025-12-26_201337_275](https://img2024.cnblogs.com/blog/2149129/202512/2149129-20251226201425085-1041224736.png)\\n\\n## 步骤一：安装Codex CLI\\n\\n选择一种安装方式即可。\\n\\nnpm（通用）\\n\\n```\\nnpm i -g @openai/codex\\n\\n# 或在有时需要的原生包名：\\n# npm i -g @openai/codex@native\\n\\ncodex --version\\n```\\n\\nHomebrew（macos系统推荐）\\n\\n```\\nbrew update\\nbrew install codex\\ncodex --version\\n```\\n\\n> 如遇 codex 无法执行或 Node 版本过旧，请升级 Node（常见需要 Node 22+），或改用 Homebrew 安装。\\n\\n## Codex配置\\n\\n> 安装后，我们开始配置codex才能通过apikey方式接入我们的API。如果你是通过openai plus会员来使用codex，可跳过后续教程，codex中直接登录plus账号即可使用。\\n\\n### 1.在第三方中转平台注册并充值\\n\\n这里我们以一个第三方中转平台作为例子，进入网站 api.v3.cm 进行注册并充值，然后在该网站 “令牌管理”页面，复制apikey，后续我们将该apikey接入到codex中使用。\\n\\n### 2.codex配置文件config.toml\\n\\nCodex 启动时会在`~/.codex/` 读取`config.toml`文件。若不存在就新建：\\n\\n```\\nmkdir -p ~/.codex\\nnano ~/.codex/config.toml\\n```\\n\\n在`config.toml`中加入如下配置，根据需求修改：\\n\\n```\\nmodel_provider = \\\"vapi\\\"  # 设置API供应商\\nmodel = \\\"gpt-5\\\"        # 填写支持codex的模型即可\\nmodel_reasoning_effort = \\\"low\\\" # 思考级别 low medium（默认） high minimal，不需要可以#注释掉\\n\\n# 供应商设置\\n[model_providers.vapi]\\nname = \\\"VAPI\\\"\\nbase_url = \\\"https://api.v3.cm/v1\\\"\\nenv_key = \\\"V_API_KEY\\\"    # 保留这个值，不需要替换为apikey\\nwire_api = \\\"chat\\\" # 使用 /v1/chat/completions 协议\\nquery_params = {}\\nrequest_max_retries = 4            # 失败最大重试次数\\nstream_max_retries = 10            # 流中断后最大重试次数\\n\\n# 可选：定义一个 profile，便于命令行快速切换\\n[profiles.vapi]\\nmodel_provider = \\\"vapi\\\"\\nmodel = \\\"gpt-5\\\"\\napproval_policy = \\\"on-request\\\"      # 需要时再询问是否执行\\nsandbox_mode = \\\"workspace-write\\\"    # 允许在当前工程写文件，依旧禁网\\n```\\n\\n### 3.添加apikey环境变量\\n\\n这步很重要，我们需要将我们设置的环境变量`V_API_KEY`添加到系统配置中。\\n\\n> key值就是我们从中转网站的 “令牌管理”页面复制的sk-开头的apikey。\\n\\n#### Window用户设置apikey环境变量：\\n\\n```\\n# 永久设置（推荐）\\nsetx V_API_KEY \\\"sk-xxxxxx\\\"\\n\\n# 临时设置，切换窗口后失效\\nset V_API_KEY=sk-xxxxxx\\n```\\n\\n#### Mac/Linux用户设置环境变量：\\n\\n#### 方法一：永久设置（推荐)\\n\\n1.编辑配置文件（或直接手动打开文件）\\n\\n```\\n# 如果使用 bash\\nnano ~/.bash_profile\\n# 或\\nnano ~/.bashrc\\n\\n# 如果使用 zsh（Mac默认）\\nnano ~/.zshrc\\n```\\n\\n2.在最后新行，添加以下内容\\n\\n```\\nexport V_API_KEY=\\\"sk-xxxxxx\\\"\\n```\\n\\n3.保存并生效\\n\\n```\\n# bash\\nsource ~/.bash_profile\\n\\n# zsh\\nsource ~/.zshrc\\n```\\n\\n#### 方法二：临时设置\\n\\n```\\nexport V_API_KEY=\\\"sk-xxxxxx\\\"\\n```\\n\\n#### 验证是否设置成功\\n\\n```\\n# Windows (CMD)\\necho %V_API_KEY%\\n\\n# Windows (PowerShell)\\necho $env:V_API_KEY\\n\\n# Mac/Linux\\necho $V_API_KEY\\n```\\n\\n如果显示`sk-xxxxxx`则说明设置成功！\\n\\n## 启动命令\\n\\n### Codex CLI原生命令\\n\\n```\\n# 直接提问\\ncodex \\\"你好\\\"\\n\\n# 选择提供商并提问(如果有多个提供商的话)\\ncodex --profile vapi \\\"你好\\\"\\n```\\n\\n### vscode中使用Codex插件\\n\\n如果是在vscode中使用codex插件，我们配置好后需要完全退出vscode重新启动才能生效。\\n\\n## Codex高级使用技巧指南\\n\\n### CLI 参考\\n\\n![Image 2: wechat_2025-12-26_201732_882](https://img2024.cnblogs.com/blog/2149129/202512/2149129-20251226201737614-2112435695.png)\\n\\n### 非交互/CI模式\\n\\n在管道中以无头方式运行 Codex。示例 GitHub Action 步骤：\\n\\n```\\n- name: Update changelog via Codex\\n  run: |\\n    npm install -g @openai/codex\\n    export OPENAI_API_KEY=\\\"${{ secrets.OPENAI_KEY }}\\\"\\n    codex exec --full-auto \\\"update CHANGELOG for next release\\\"\\n```\\n\\n### 模型上下文协议（MCP）\\n\\n通过在`~/.codex/config.toml`中定义一个`mcp_servers`部分，可以将 Codex CLI 配置为使用 MCP 服务器。它旨在反映 Claude 和 Cursor 等工具在其各自的 JSON 配置文件中的 mcpServers 定义方式，尽管 Codex 的格式略有不同，因为它使用的是 TOML 而不是 JSON，例如：\\n\\n```\\n# IMPORTANT: the top-level key is `mcp_servers` rather than `mcpServers`.\\n[mcp_servers.server-name]\\ncommand = \\\"npx\\\"\\nargs = [\\\"-y\\\", \\\"mcp-server\\\"]\\nenv = { \\\"API_KEY\\\" = \\\"value\\\" }\\n```\\n\\n## 参考资料\\n\\nCodex官方详细配置说明文档\\n\\n### 如果对您有所帮助，欢迎您点个关注，我会定时更新技术文档，大家一起讨论学习，一起进步。\\n\\n_![Image Codex CLI：\\n\\n   ```\\n   npm install -g @openai/codex\\n   ```\\n2. 准备 OpenAI API Key\\n3. 确保你已有的中转接口兼容 OpenAI 格式，\\n\\n---\\n\\n### 📌 二、设置 PowerShell 环境变量\\n\\n```\\n# 设置你的 API Key（使用哪家的看你的代理）\\n$env:OPENAI_API_KEY = \\\"sk-...\\\"\\n\\n# 设置中转 API 地址（结尾一定是 /v1）\\n$env:OPENAI_BASE_URL = \\\"https://你的代理地址/v1\\\"\\n```\\n\\n📌 示例（如图）：\\n\\n```\\n$env:OPENAI_API_KEY = \\\"sk-proj-...\\\"\\n$env:OPENAI_BASE_URL = \\\"https://api.zhangsan.cool/v1\\\"\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n### 📌 三、如何检验 Codex CLI 是否配置成功\\n\\n分别执行以下命令检查是否配置成功：\\n\\n```\\ncodex --version\\necho $env:OPENAI_API_KEY\\necho $env:OPENAI_BASE_URL\\n```\\n\\n若以上三条命令均能正常输出，说明 Codex CLI 与环境变量已配置成功。\\n\\n### 📌 四、稳定使用方式\\n\\n在 `codex-main` 文件夹内新建 `start.ps1`（：\\n\\n```\\n$env:OPENAI_API_KEY = \\\"sk-proj-...\\\"\\n$env:OPENAI_BASE_URL = \\\"https://api.zhangsan.cool/v1\\\"\\ncodex --model gpt-5.2\\n```\\n\\n> 若模型名称不生效，请以中转接口实际支持的模型为准。\\n\\n以后只需在执行 `start.ps1`，就能进入 Codex CLI 会话。\\n\\n### ### Q32

"{\"title\":\"CC Switch 一键切换 API | 菜鸟教程\",\"description\":\"CC Switch 一键切换 API  随着 Claude Code、OpenAI Codex、Gemini CLI 等 AI 编程工具的流行，开发者往往需要在多个 API 提供商之间频繁切换——官方 API、国内镜像、第三方代理……每次手动修改配置文件既繁琐又容易出错。  CC Switch 就是解决这个痛点的桌面工具，它提供一个统一的图形界面，让你可以：                             功能         ..\",\"url\":\"https://www.runoob.com/ai-agent/cc-switch.html\",\"content\":\"随着 Claude Code、OpenAI Codex、Gemini CLI 等 AI 编程工具的流行，开发者往往需要在多个 API 提供商之间频繁切换——官方 API、国内镜像、第三方代理……每次手动修改配置文件既繁琐又容易出错。\\n\\nCC Switch 就是解决这个痛点的桌面工具，它提供一个统一的图形界面，让你可以：\\n\\n| 功能 | 说明 |\\n| --- | --- |\\n| 🔄 一键切换 Provider | 保存多套 API 配置，点一下即可切换，无需手动编辑 JSON |\\n| 🖥️ 多应用统一管理 | 同时管理 Claude Code、Codex、Gemini CLI、OpenCode、OpenClaw 五款工具 |\\n| 🛡️ 本地 API 代理 | 内置高性能 HTTP 代理，支持自动故障转移与请求监控 |\\n| 🔌 MCP 服务器管理 | 可视化添加、编辑和同步 MCP 服务器配置 |\\n| 📊 用量统计 | 实时查看 Token 消耗与 API 费用 |\\n| 💾 备份与恢复 | 自动备份配置，防止误操作导致数据丢失 |\\n\\n### 支持管理的应用\\n\\n- 🤖 __Claude Code__（Anthropic）\\n- ⚡ __Codex__（OpenAI）\\n- ✨ __Gemini CLI__（Google）\\n- 🧩 __OpenCode__（开源）\\n- 🦅 __OpenClaw__（第五款应用，v3.11.0 新增）\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 安装\\n\\n前往 GitHub Releases: https://github.com/farion1231/cc-switch/releases/latest 下载对应平台的安装包。\\n\\n拉到网页最底部，资源包部分，下载对应平台的安装包：\\n\\n![Image 1](https://www.runoob.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/31595d37-9f05-4d9d-90d9-c17c2b119da6.png)\\n\\n### 系统要求\\n\\n| 系统 | 最低版本 | 架构 |\\n| --- | --- | --- |\\n| Windows | Windows 10 及以上 | x64 |\\n| macOS | macOS 10.15 (Catalina) 及以上 | Intel (x64) / Apple Silicon (arm64) |\\n| Linux | 见下表 | x64 / arm64 |\\n\\n### Windows\\n\\n| 文件 | 说明 |\\n| --- | --- |\\n| `CC-Switch-vX.X.X-Windows.msi` | ✅ __推荐__——MSI 安装包，支持自动更新 |\\n| `CC-Switch-vX.X.X-Windows-Portable.zip` | 便携版，解压即用，不写注册表 |\\n\\n双击 MSI 文件，按向导完成安装后，在开始菜单搜索\\\"CC Switch\\\"启动即可。\\n\\n> ⚠️ Windows 版本已禁用\\\"一键安装\\\"功能，以避免协议处理器的副作用。如需安装 Claude Code 等工具，请手动安装后再通过 CC Switch 管理。\\n\\n### macOS\\n\\n__方式一：直接下载（推荐）__\\n\\n1. 下载 `CC-Switch-vX.X.X-macOS.zip`\\n2. 解压后将 `CC Switch.app` 拖入「应用程序」文件夹\\n3. 首次启动时右键点击 → 打开，或前往「系统设置 → 隐私与安全性 → 仍要打开」\\n\\n> ⚠️ 由于作者没有 Apple 开发者账号，macOS 可能提示\\\"未知开发者\\\"。关闭提示后，前往「系统设置 → 隐私与安全性」点击「仍要打开」，之后每次均可正常启动。\\n\\n__方式二：Homebrew__\\n\\n```\\n# 添加 tap 并安装\\nbrew tap farion1231/ccswitch\\nbrew install --cask cc-switch\\n# 更新\\nbrew upgrade --cask cc-switch\\n```\\n\\n### Linux\\n\\n根据发行版选择对应格式：\\n\\n| 发行版 | 推荐格式 | 安装命令 |\\n| --- | --- | --- |\\n| Ubuntu / Debian / Mint | `.deb` | `sudo apt install ./CC-Switch-*.deb` |\\n| Fedora / RHEL / Rocky | `.rpm` | `sudo dnf install ./CC-Switch-*.rpm` |\\n| openSUSE | `.rpm` | `sudo zypper install ./CC-Switch-*.rpm` |\\n| Arch / Manjaro / 其他 | `.AppImage` | 见下方 |\\n\\nAppImage 使用方式：\\n\\n```\\nchmod +x CC-Switch-*.AppImage\\n./CC-Switch-*.AppImage\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 快速上手\\n\\n安装完成后，按以下步骤完成首次配置：\\n\\n__第一步：启动 CC Switch__\\n\\n首次启动时，CC Switch 会自动检测已安装的 CLI 工具并尝试导入现有配置，系统托盘中会出现 CC Switch 图标。\\n\\n__第二步：选择要管理的应用__\\n\\n主界面顶部是应用切换栏，点击对应图标（Claude Code / Codex / Gemini CLI 等）即可切换当前管理的应用，你可以在设置中隐藏不需要的应用。\\n\\n![Image 2](https://www.runoob.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/3f634726-617d-43a7-9040-a4acaaca9433.png)\\n\\n__第三步：添加第一个 Provider__\\n\\n点击右上角的 + ，从内置预设中选择（如官方 Anthropic API、DeepSeek、阿里百炼等），或手动填写以下信息：\\n\\n![Image 3](https://www.runoob.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/6e4e3a28-1f43-446a-a939-c08eb195ba43.png)\\n\\n- __名称__：便于区分的备注名\\n- __API Key__：服务商提供的密钥\\n- __Base URL__（可选）：自定义代理地址\\n- __模型__：指定默认使用的模型名称\\n- __API 格式__：Anthropic Messages 原生格式 或 OpenAI Chat Completions 兼容格式\\n\\n![Image 4](https://www.runoob.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/11305e0d-1ca7-46f6-83d6-cec24c4f46bd.png)\\n\\n__第四步：切换 Provider__\\n\\n在列表中点击目标 Provider，再点击「启用」，CC Switch 会自动将配置写入对应 CLI 工具的配置文件:\\n\\n![Image 5](https://www.runoob.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/0d05c29d-f2c4-4c42-8afc-88ed80937a30.png)\\n\\n在终端直接运行 `claude` 命令时，即会使用新配置。\\n\\n__第五步：验证配置（可选）__\\n\\n点击 Provider 旁的「健康检查」按钮，发送一个测试请求验证 API Key 和网络连通性。\\n\\n![Image 6](https://www.runoob.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/a0a56b4b-ee98-42db-8e26-13a46b112455.png)\\n\\n> 💡 按 `Cmd/Ctrl + ,` 快速打开设置；按 `ESC` 关闭当前面板。\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 进阶功能\\n\\n![Image 7](https://www.runoob.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/856e0b03-c02d-4213-b4e8-ea52dba3c5fa.png)\\n\\n### MCP 服务器管理\\n\\n在「MCP」面板中，可视化添加、编辑和删除 MCP（Model Context Protocol）服务器。配置自动同步到对应 CLI 工具，支持从已安装的应用一键导入现有 MCP 配置。\\n\\n![Image 8](https://www.runoob.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/60c0a1c3-73c2-4749-a567-34c5f4587360.png)\\n\\n### Skills 管理\\n\\nSkills 是 Claude Code 的提示词增强功能。CC Switch 支持：\\n\\n- 从 GitHub 仓库安装 Skills（内置 `baoyu-skills` 等预设仓库）\\n- 从本地 ZIP 文件安装\\n- 管理 Claude Code 和 Codex 的 Skills\\n\\n![Image 9](https://www.runoob.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/667edee0-4fcb-4d17-ae1d-233f2ab5c4d6.png)\\n\\n### 会话管理器（Session Manager）\\n\\n在「会话」页面，可浏览全部五个应用的历史对话记录，支持目录导航和会话内搜索，并自动按当前应用过滤显示。\\n\\n![Image 10](https://www.runoob.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/2e35294b-31e0-4777-8fe4-20116a0ee801.png)\\n\\n### 备份管理\\n\\nCC Switch 会定期自动备份数据库，在数据库迁移前也会自动备份。在「设置 → 备份管理」中可以：查看所有备份、重命名、删除，以及手动创建备份。\\n\\n### 用量统计与计费\\n\\n「用量」页面展示 Token 消耗统计，支持自动刷新、缓存命中率分析，以及按模型和 Provider 分类查看费用，帮助掌控 API 开支。\\n\\n### Claude Rectifier（思考签名修复）\\n\\n当使用某些第三方 API 网关时，Claude 的 thinking block 格式可能不兼容。开启 Rectifier 后，代理层会自动修复此问题。可在「设置 → 高级」中开关。\\n\\n### WebDAV 自动同步\\n\\n支持将数据库同步到 WebDAV 服务（如坚果云），实现多设备间的配置共享。内置大文件保护机制，防止误传。\",\"metadata\":{\"keywords\":\"CC Switch 一键切换 API\",\"viewport\":\"width=device-width, initial-scale=1.0\",\"apple-mobile-web-app-title\":\"菜鸟教程\",\"description\":\"CC Switch 一键切换 API  随着 Claude Code、OpenAI Codex、Gemini CLI 等 AI 编程工具的流行，开发者往往需要在多个 API 提供商之间频繁切换——官方 API、国内镜像、第三方代理……每次手动修改配置文件既繁琐又容易出错。  CC Switch 就是解决这个痛点的桌面工具，它提供一个统一的图形界面，让你可以：                             功能         ..\",\"robots\":\"max-image-preview:large\",\"lang\":\"zh-Hans\"},\"external\":{\"stylesheet\":{\"/wp-content/themes/runoob/assets/feedback/stable/2.0/feedback.css?1.0\":{},\"https://static.char123.com/assets/simditor/2.3.6/styles/simditor.min.css\":{\"type\":\"text/css\"},\"https://www.runoob.com/wp-content/themes/runoob/style.css?v=1.125\":{\"media\":\"all\"},\"/wp-content/themes/runoob/assets/css/qa.css?1.45\":{},\"https://static.jyshare.com/assets/font-awesome-4.7.0/css/font-awesome.min.css\":{\"media\":\"all\"},\"https://static.char123.com/assets/upvotejs/dist/upvotejs/upvotejs.css\":{}},\"shortcut\":{\"https://static.char123.com/images/favicon.ico\":{}},\"canonical\":{\"http://www.runoob.com/ai-agent/cc-switch.html\":{}},\"apple-touch-icon\":{\"https://static.char123.com/images/icon/mobile-icon.png\":{}}}}"

### Q46

### Main Response  
图片中错误信息的具体内容为：  

```
启动对话时出错
invalid configuration: `wire_api = "chat"` is no longer supported.
How to fix: set `wire_api = "responses"` in your provider config.
More info: https://github.com/openai/codex/discussions/7782
in `model_providers.custom.wire_api`
```  


### Detailed Observations  
- 错误信息以粉色背景的弹窗形式呈现，位于图像上方区域。  
- 文字包含中英文混合内容：首行是中文提示“启动对话时出错”，后续为英文的错误描述、修复建议和参考链接。  
- 英文部分明确指出配置错误（`wire_api = "chat"` 不再支持），并给出修复方案（将 `wire_api` 设为 `"responses"`）及 GitHub 讨论链接（用于获取更多信息）。  


### Additional Notes  
- 图像下方还有一行中文提问“我们应该在 vizion-lab 中构建什么？”，但这不属于错误信息内容，是独立于错误弹窗的文本。  
- 错误弹窗右上角有关闭按钮（“×”符号），表明这是可交互的提示窗口。

先睹为快\\n\\n📚 2 - 容器沙盒应用\\n\\nlz\\n\\nfz\\n\\nalpine\\n\\nubuntu\\n\\nkali\\n\\ndebian\\n\\nfedora\\n\\ndaemon\\n\\nmirror\\n\\nsetup\\n\\nsnapx\\n\\nimage\\n\\nimages\\n\\necpull\\n\\ncontainer\\n\\nps\\n\\nrun\\n\\nexec\\n\\nnetwork\\n\\ninfo\\n\\ninit\\n\\ncfg\\n\\ncur\\n\\n### x webtop\\n\\n--init\\n\\n--cfg\\n\\n--cur\\n\\nls\\n\\nopen\\n\\nhome\\n\\nimage\\n\\ncontainer\\n\\nrun\\n\\nps\\n\\nstop\\n\\nrestart\\n\\nstart\\n\\nrm\\n\\npause\\n\\nunpause\\n\\nbash\\n\\nall\\n\\n### x sb\\n\\n📚 1 - 快速上手\\n\\nairrun\\n\\neditrun\\n\\nstdiorun\\n\\nx lsio\\n\\n## Shell\\n\\nx nu\\n\\nx elv\\n\\nx fish\\n\\nx onsh\\n\\nx tcsh\\n\\nx pwsh\\n\\n## 编程语言\\n\\nx zig\\n\\nx lua\\n\\nx luarocks\\n\\n## Python\\n\\nx python\\n\\nx pip\\n\\n## JS\\n\\nx node\\n\\nx npm\\n\\nx npx\\n\\nx deno\\n\\nx bun\\n\\n## Perl & Raku\\n\\nx perl\\n\\nx raku\\n\\n## Java\\n\\nx java\\n\\nx groovy\\n\\n查看源码\\n\\n大纲\\n\\n![Image 3: 微信公众号](https://cn.x-cmd.com/imgs/wechat_qrcode.webp)\\n\\noh my x\\n\\n扫码关注官方微信公众号获取开源软件和 x-cmd 的最新用法与独家资讯\\n\\n# `x codex zhipu` - 告别 ChatGPT 限制，Codex 零门槛接入智谱 GLM 模型 ​\\n\\n在默认情况下，Codex 使用的是 ChatGPT 系列模型（如 GPT-4、GPT-3.5）。然而，对于国内用户来说，访问 ChatGPT 可能存在网络或账号限制。此时，你可以选择接入智谱 AI 提供的 GLM 系列模型。\\n\\n智谱 AI 是国内领先的大模型提供商之一，其 GLM 模型（如 GLM-4.5、GLM-4.5-Air、GLM-4.5-Flash 等）在通用理解、代码生成、知识问答等任务上都有出色表现。通过 `x codex zhipu` 命令，你可以让 Codex 切换到智谱模型，从而在国内环境享受更快的响应、更低的网络门槛（无需翻墙或海外账号）！\\n\\n## 获取智谱 API key ​\\n\\n1. 访问其智谱 AI 开放平台进行注册。\\n2. 注册账号后，在 “API Keys” 页面创建新的密钥。建议为不同项目创建不同的 API Key，便于管理与追踪使用情况。\\n\\n## 启动 codex 并接入智谱 AI 模型 ​\\n\\n获取 Key 后，可将其导入 x-cmd 的配置或通过环境变量方式启用。\\n\\n- 配置并启动示例：\\n\\nbash\\n\\n```\\n# 配置智谱 API key\\nx zhipu init\\n# 也可以通过 x-cmd 配置智谱的 API Key -> x zhipu --cfg apikey=\\\"<your_api>\\\"\\n\\n# 启动 codex 并接入智谱 AI 模型\\nx codex zhipu\\n```\\n\\n- 或者使用环境变量方式：\\n\\nbash\\n\\n```\\nexport CODEX_API_KEY=\\\"<your_zhipu_api_key>\\\"\\nx codex zhipu\\n```\\n\\n配置完成后，codex 将使用智谱模型处理你的编程请求。工作方式与原生 ChatGPT 模型几乎一致，整个代理流程、命令行交互逻辑依旧保持一致，你无需学习新的交互方式。\\n\\nPager\\n\\n上一篇📚 2 - 接入 DeepSeek\\n\\n下一篇 📚 4 - Agent Skill\\n\\n帮助我们改善文档\\n\\nX-CMD 的文档内容来自命令的帮助文档、多个数据源以及文档库生成。文档中如果有错误或不明确的地方，欢迎通过这些方式进行告知~\\n\\n![Image 4: qywx-qrcode](https://cn.x-cmd.com/image/qywx.webp) 扫描添加小助手  \\n完成验证加入微信群\\n\\n提交 GitHub issue 反馈  提交 GitCode issue 反馈  提交 Gitee issue 反馈\\n\\n![Image 5: x-cmd-logo](https://cn.x-cmd.com/dark-logo.svg)\\n\\nX-CMD\\n\\nAGPL 3.0 Licensed\\n\\n导航\\n\\n- 模块\\n- 包\\n- 主题\\n- Install\\n- Zig\\n- Go\\n- LCTT\\n- 命令手册\\n\\n关于\\n\\n- 导论\\n- 关于中山\\n- 关于我们\\n- 联系我们\\n- 常见问题\\n- 变更日志\\n\\n资源\\n\\n- GitHub\\n- Gitee\\n- Codeberg\\n- GitCode\\n- 博客\\n\\n© 2022-2026 李均豪(Li Junhao) 版权所有中山市栩栩云生科技有限公司 提供服务支持\\n\\n粤ICP备2021166893号-1\",\"metadata\":{\"application-name\":\"X-CMD\",\"og:image\":\"https://cn.x-cmd.com/icons/apple-touch-120x120.png\",\"og:type\":\"website\",\"theme-color\":\"#191919\",\"twitter:card\":\"summary_large_image\",\"og:site_name\":\"X-CMD\",\"google-site-verification\":\"_18qqOuIfzijSG7HV3nMHul_TLcD5hnpJBB0m3omcN0\",\"keywords\":\"X-CMD, x-cmd, CLI, 命令行工具, 包管理工具, 命令行主题, 编程语言配置安装, 命令行数据处理\",\"author\":\"X-CMD\",\"apple-mobile-web-app-title\":\"X-CMD\",\"og:title\":\"x codex zhipu -
